The lay Sister, unedified, told another Sister that Thérèse was not a saint, not even a good religious. Thérèse heard of it and was so happy she confided it to a Sister, who later told Pauline it was her most edifying memory of Thérèse. Later cards give more detail. At the end of July 1897 Thérèse had an unconquerable disgust for food. Sr Saint-Vincent de Paul, named as the lay Sister, brought her a cup of meat juice. Thérèse refused, for lack of appetite and because the prioress had not ordered it. The Sister went back to the kitchen hurt and said Thérèse was not only not a saint but not even a good religious. Told of it, Thérèse rejoiced ("what joy to hear on my deathbed that I am not a good religious"). She was less pleased by the charge that she had known neither suffering nor humiliation: "And I who have suffered so much since my earliest childhood!"
- Cards: F-EW-1227, F-EW-1436, F-EW-1438
- Best details:
  - « Sœur Thérèse de l'Enfant Jésus non seulement n'est pas une sainte, mais elle n'est pas même une bonne religieuse. » (F-EW-1227).
  - Pauline does not recall whether Thérèse drank it in the end (F-EW-1227).
  - The lay Sister is named: Sr Saint-Vincent de Paul (F-EW-1436, F-EW-1438).
  - Refused because the prioress had not ordered it (F-EW-1438).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (partly saw, partly heard from the confidante; 1910). Medium. | Mère Agnès (heard it reported; Process deposition as cited by editors); Thérèse (DE); TSA (heard Thérèse tell it; Carmel archive biography). High.
- Conflicts: Pauline (1910) says the lay Sister was unnamed and that Thérèse said it would make her vomit. The St-Augustin circular (1929) tells it without naming the critic (K-F183); the St-Augustin biography names Sr Saint-Vincent de Paul. F-EW-1336 (TSA) reports a very similar phrase from an unnamed nun; they are probably the same remark.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high: a criticism from inside the community that made her happy.

### E-P6-235 — 31 July 1897, infirmary — "I have two boots but no sack yet": Puss in Boots
- What happened: She told the story of "the miller and his three sons" and said "I have two boots but no sack yet! That means I am not near dying." (The editors' note: it is the story of "Puss in Boots", not a fable of La Fontaine.) She asked "How will baby do to die? What will I die of?"
- Cards: F-OW-560
- Best details:
  - "I have two boots but no sack yet! That means I am not near dying." (F-OW-560).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 31.7). High. No match in Novissima Verba by keyword. The Carmel editors correct "La Fontaine" to "Puss in Boots".
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-236 — 31 July 1897 (night after a big spitting of blood), infirmary — "I will be a little thief"; "The Thief will come... Alleluia!"
- What happened: On 31 July she said: "Yes I will fly... Many things will disappear from Heaven that I will bring you... I will be a little thief, I will take everything that pleases me..." Looking at the statue of the Virgin and pointing to her little basin: "When it came last night (a big spitting of blood) I thought you were going to take me away!" Mère Agnès, who had fallen asleep watching her, was told: "Peter, James and John!..." (the sleeping apostles). She sang: "The Thief will come / and carry me off / Alleluia!" She said: "I thought I must be very nice and wait for the Thief very kindly." And: "Do not chat with each other, it is quite enough to look at each other." "I tell you I have a long time yet if the Blessed Virgin does not put her hand to it!"
- Cards: F-OW-561
- Best details:
  - « Je serai une petite voleuse, je prendrai tout ce qui me plaira... » (F-OW-561).
  - « L'Voleur viendra / Et m'emport'ra / Alleluia ! » (F-OW-561).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 31.7.5-12). High. Novissima Verba has "Peter, James and John" and "thief" sayings; "little thief" is new. Her big spitting of blood on the night 30-31 July is recorded here.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial; yes for the "little thief" line
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-237 — 31 July 1897, infirmary — "I found happiness and joy on earth, but only in suffering": the spark under the ashes
- What happened: On 31 July she said: "I have found happiness and joy on earth, but only in suffering, for I have suffered much here below; this must be made known to souls..." She explained: "Since my first Communion, since I asked Jesus to change for me into bitterness all the consolations of the earth, I had a perpetual desire to suffer. Yet I did not think of making it my joy; that is a grace given me only later. Until then it was like a spark hidden under ashes, and like the flowers of a tree that must become fruits in their time. But seeing my flowers always fall, that is, letting myself go to tears when I suffered, I said to myself with surprise and sadness: but it will never be more than desires!"
- Cards: F-OW-562
- Best details:
  - « J'ai trouvé le bonheur et la joie sur la terre, mais uniquement dans la souffrance, car j'ai beaucoup souffert ici-bas ; il faudra le faire savoir aux âmes... » (F-OW-562).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 31.7.13). High. "Found happiness" is not in Novissima Verba. Marie des Anges dates the saying "31-7" (F-EW-2523, added to E-P6-226).
- Conflicts: A dated instruction to the recorder ("must be made known"). Desire to suffer since first Communion (1884); joy in it a later grace.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-238 — 31 July 1897, infirmary — The doctor says "a month or more": "the good God has deceived everybody"
- What happened: On 31 July she said: "This evening, when you told me that M. de Cornière thought I had a month or more, I could not believe it; it was such a great difference from yesterday when he said they had to administer me that very day! But it left me in deep calm. What does it matter to me to remain a long time on earth! If I suffer much and ever more, I have no fear; the good God will give me strength; he will not abandon me." A Sister said "If you live a long time, nobody will understand anything!" She answered: "What does it matter! Everybody can well despise me; that is what I always wanted; I will have it at the end of my life!" And: "Now that the good God has done what he wanted to do, that he has deceived everybody... He will come like a thief at the hour when one no longer thinks of it; that is my little idea."
- Cards: F-OW-563
- Best details:
  - « Tout le monde peut bien me mépriser, c'est toujours ce que j'ai désiré ; je l'aurai à la fin de ma vie ! » (F-OW-563).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 31.7.14-16). High. LT 263 (10 Aug) gives the doctor's view of 30 July from her side.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-239 — 1897, infirmary, near the end — "Ah! you still have desires!"
- What happened: Céline said she would like to keep a holy picture that belonged to Thérèse. Thérèse answered: "Ah! you still have desires!" After her death Céline should ask for nothing of hers and take what she was given. Only in heaven would they have the right to own.
- Cards: F-EW-1258
- Best details:
  - « Ah! vous avez encore des désirs!... » (F-EW-1258).
- Evidence: Céline (heard, 1910). High. Note: Céline later became keeper of the relics (F-EW-1758).
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium: gentle irony.

### E-P6-240 — infirmary, 1897 — "Perhaps it is not allowed": the sheet on her feet
- What happened: On her deathbed, burning with fever, Marie wanted to take the sheet off her feet. Thérèse said it was perhaps not allowed. Marie's 1910 account adds that in her last days, burning with fever, Marie wanted to lift the sheet off her feet to cool them. Thérèse asked whether it was perhaps not permitted. Gonzague had once said it was better to keep the wool blanket on even in summer.
- Cards: F-EW-1274, F-EW-1594
- Best details:
  - « Ce n'est peut-être pas permis. » (F-EW-1274).
  - Gonzague's earlier rule: keep the wool blanket on even in summer (F-EW-1594).
- Evidence: Marie (present, 1915). High. | Marie (saw and heard, 1910). High.
- Conflicts: Compare Céline's blanket story (F-EW-1250).
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-241 — infirmary, 1897 — "She will be so busy with me she won't manage it all"
- What happened: Near the end, Marie said she would struggle to console Mother Agnès. Thérèse said not to worry: Agnès would have no time to think of her sorrow, since she would be so busy with Thérèse till the end of her life that she could not keep up. Marie's 1910 account dates it to July 1897 in the infirmary.
- Cards: F-EW-1275, F-EW-1428
- Best details:
  - « jusqu'à la fin de sa vie, elle sera si occupée de moi, qu'elle ne pourra même pas suffire à tout » (F-EW-1275).
  - « jusqu'à la fin de sa vie elle sera si occupée de moi qu'elle ne pourra même pas suffire à tout » (F-EW-1428).
- Evidence: Marie (heard, 1915). High. Marie treats it as prophetic. | Marie (heard, 1910). High. Presented as a prophecy fulfilled.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-242 — 1897 — "I never did my own will on earth"
- What happened: At the end of her life Pauline heard her say she had never done her own will on earth, and so God would do all her wills in heaven. The Carnet jaune dates the saying to 13 July: "God will have to do all my wishes in Heaven, because I never did my own will on earth."
- Cards: F-EW-1242, F-OW-260
- Best details:
  - "I never did my own will on earth" (F-EW-1242).
  - Date 13 July (F-OW-260).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High. | Mère Agnès (CJ 13.7). Medium.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-243 — summer 1897, infirmary — "You will find only a little skeleton"
- What happened: Her sisters said how happy they would be if her body were preserved. She said they would find only a small skeleton, as was found of St Aloysius Gonzaga. Céline's account (F-EW-1452, F-EW-1454) dates it 8 July 1897. Céline said God would do wonders for her and they would find her body incorrupt. Thérèse answered with sadness, as if hurt: not that marvel; it would leave her little way of humility. "Little souls" must have nothing to envy her; expect to find only a skeleton. She said near the end there should be nothing but the ordinary in her whole life. Céline says this was verified at the exhumation of 6 September 1910.
- Cards: F-EW-1245, F-EW-1452, F-EW-1454
- Best details:
  - « Vous ne retrouverez de moi qu'un petit squelette » (F-EW-1245).
  - « il faut que 'les petites âmes' ne puissent rien m'envier » (F-EW-1452).
  - « attendez-vous donc à ne retrouver de moi qu'un squelette » (F-EW-1454).
- Evidence: Marie (heard, 1910). High. Céline gives a fuller version (F-EW-1452, later chunk). The 1910 exhumation found bones only. | Céline (heard; 1910, 1915). High. Matches Marie (F-EW-1245).
- Conflicts: Marie gives it as sisters hoping the body would be preserved, dated summer 1897; Céline dates 8 July.
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-244 — infirmary, 1897 — "I'm un-nailing Him"
- What happened: She caressed her crucifix with flowers. One day Céline saw her carefully touching its crown of thorns and nails and asked what she was doing. Embarrassed at being caught, she said she was un-nailing Him and taking off His crown of thorns. Céline adds that she threw flowers to the great Christ in the courtyard and in her illness covered her crucifix with roses, removing faded petals. Céline once saw her touching the thorns and nails with her fingertips: "I am unnailing him and taking off his crown of thorns."
- Cards: F-EW-1291, F-EW-1358
- Best details:
  - « Je le décloue et je lui enlève la couronne d'épines. » (F-EW-1291).
  - « Je le décloue et je lui enlève sa couronne d'épines. » (F-EW-1358, Céline's Conseils et Souvenirs).
- Evidence: Céline (saw, 1915). High. Scene S-EW-047. | Céline (saw and heard). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-245 — infirmary, 1897 — "The good thief stole it": heaven by a ruse of love
- What happened: She made no account of her works and said with her usual charm that her protectors in heaven were those who stole it, like the Holy Innocents and the good thief. Great saints earned heaven by works, but she, a very little soul, wanted it "by a ruse, a ruse of love".
- Cards: F-EW-1290
- Best details:
  - « Mes protecteurs au ciel sont ceux qui l'ont volé, comme les saints Innocents et le bon larron » (F-EW-1290).
- Evidence: Céline (heard, 1915). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-246 — last months, infirmary — "All is well, all is accomplished, only love counts"
- What happened: In her last months, "like Our Lord before dying", she told Céline in a grave tone that all was well, all was accomplished, only love counts. Céline's Conseils et Souvenirs put the saying on the eve of death, 29 Sept 1897: "All is well, all is accomplished, only love counts." The time of day is not given.
- Cards: F-EW-1295, F-EW-1648, F-EW-1649
- Best details:
  - « Tout est bien, tout est accompli, c'est l'amour seul qui compte » (F-EW-1295).
  - Placed on 29 Sept 1897 (F-EW-1648).
- Evidence: Céline (heard, 1915). High. | Céline (heard; CS §VII opening). High.
- Conflicts: PA 1915 (F-EW-1295) says "in her last months".
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-247 — 1897 — "I will come and undeceive you if my way is false"
- What happened: She promised her novices not to leave them in error if her way were false, and to come and undeceive them. People linked this to words reported in an apparition to Mother Carmela of Gallipoli: "My way is sure, and I was not mistaken in following it." Bishop Giannattasio (Gallipoli) learned afterwards that the same wording had been said by Thérèse to three novices about her "little way": "I will come back to tell you if I was wrong and if my way is sure" (he cites HA 12). In his view she kept the promise in the Gallipoli apparition. The editors' introduction ties the phrase to the promise to Sr Marie de la Trinité: if in heaven she found she had misled her, she would come at once and warn her. He said he wrote the phrase on his card with no plan to provoke a miracle, but he was "invincibly determined" to draw attention to it.
- Cards: F-EW-1288, F-EW-2470
- Best details:
  - « Ma voie est sûre et je ne me suis pas trompée en la suivant » (F-EW-1288).
  - "invincibly determined" to draw attention to the phrase (F-EW-2470).
- Evidence: Céline (1915). Medium: the Gallipoli apparition (1910, not dated in the card) is second-hand. | Giannattasio (PO 33 [1327v]-[1328r]); editors' introduction. Medium: an interpretation, not an observation.
- Conflicts: The editors say this is not a different event but another witness of the same promise.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-248 — undated; second part 1897, infirmary — "I hide my pains from God; I tell Mary everything"
- What happened: Thérèse said, pleasantly, that she liked to hide her pains from God, so as to look happy with all He does. She hid nothing from the Blessed Virgin. In her last illness, after a calmer hour, Mère Agnès asked if she had suffered less. She said no, just as much, "but I complained to the Blessed Virgin".
- Cards: F-EW-1296
- Best details:
  - "but I complained to the Blessed Virgin" (F-EW-1296).
- Evidence: MT (1916), the illness scene probably heard from Agnès or others: medium (MT not present in the infirmary then).
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-249 — 1897, kitchen and infirmary — The soup she did not want; a message for MM
- What happened: MM once made a soup specially for Thérèse, who reproached her. When MM did it again a few days later, Thérèse showed real pain. She was very ill then. MM never saw her in a bad mood with her. On her deathbed Thérèse told Mère Agnès to tell MM that in heaven she would pray for her and love her as much as the other novices. Marie-Madeleine confirms the deathbed message. Marthe says the words were addressed to "our Mother". When Thérèse was already very ill, Marie-Madeleine made a soup specially for her. Thérèse reproached her, saying the community soup was enough. She also told the lay Sisters: "you should never taste a dish needlessly."
- Cards: F-EW-1304, F-EW-1305, F-EW-1339, F-EW-1340
- Best details:
  - « Vous lui direz qu'au ciel je prierai pour elle, et je l'aimerai autant que les autres novices. » (F-EW-1304).
  - « Vous lui direz qu'au Ciel, je prierai pour elle, et que je l'aimerai autant que les autres novices » (F-EW-1339).
- Evidence: MM (Process 1911; message relayed by Agnès). Medium-high. | Marie-Madeleine (told by Mère Agnès); Marthe; two witnesses. High.
- Conflicts: Marthe says the words were addressed to "our Mother" (F-EW-1339).
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-250 — 1897, Lisieux Carmel — "You may have the grace of martyrdom"
- What happened: Near the end, alluding to signs of coming persecution, Thérèse told TSA that TSA was happier than she. Thérèse was going to heaven, but TSA might have the grace of martyrdom. TSA's own notes give the same saying: near the end Thérèse told her, you are happier than me; I'm going to heaven, but you may have the grace of martyrdom.
- Cards: F-EW-1313, F-EW-1344
- Best details:
  - Singular "you" in PO, plural "you [nuns] are happier" in PA (F-EW-1313).
  - TSA notes, CHARITE pour DIEU (F-EW-1344).
- Evidence: TSA (heard, 1911 and 1915). High. | TSA (notes). High.
- Conflicts: PO singular versus PA plural address.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-251 — summer 1897, infirmary — "No, no, one must let Him act": refusing prayers for relief
- What happened: Fearing sharper pain for her, TSA said she would pray God to soften it. Thérèse answered quickly: "No, no, one must let Him act." In 1915 TSA links this to her statement that in the last months the prioress "thought she ought to refuse to use the means of relieving her." TSA's own notes: in the last months, as pains grew violent and the prioress refused to use means to relieve her, TSA wanted to ask God to ease her. Thérèse said quickly: no, no, let Him act; "not for the reward, to please Him".
- Cards: F-EW-1314, F-EW-1612
- Best details:
  - « Non, non, il faut le laisser faire » (F-EW-1314).
  - « Non, non, il faut Le laisser faire ! ... Pas pour la récompense, pour Lui faire plaisir » (F-EW-1612).
- Evidence: TSA (heard Thérèse; the prioress's refusal is community knowledge). High for the words, medium for the refusal. | TSA (heard; notes, TEMPERANCE). High.
- Conflicts: Who blocked pain relief: TSA blames the prioress (PA [834]); MdA says God "permitted" that the doctor did not think of it (PO-14 [604r]) or "could not" give it (PA-10 [899]). See gaps. | TSA again attributes the refusal of relief to the prioress (see E-P6-308).
- New to English readers: partial (the prioress's refusal is new)
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-252 — summer 1897, infirmary — Deathbed joy at being called "not even a good religious"
- What happened: TSA visited Thérèse in her last illness and found her radiant. Thérèse explained that a Sister had visited and said, "If you knew how little you are loved and appreciated!" A few days earlier she had also heard a Sister tell another: "I don't know why people talk so much about Sr Thérèse; she does nothing remarkable; we don't see her practise virtue; one cannot even say she is a good religious." Thérèse said: to hear on her deathbed that she was not a good religious, what joy! She told TSA this made her very happy.
- Cards: F-EW-1315, F-EW-1336
- Best details:
  - « on ne peut même pas dire qu'elle soit une bonne religieuse! » (F-EW-1315).
  - TSA (1911): the second speaker said it "in a fit of bad temper" (F-EW-1315).
  - The visiting Sister's words "how little you are loved and appreciated here" appear in the Souvenirs (F-EW-1336).
- Evidence: TSA, heard from Thérèse (Process 1911 and 1915; Carmel archive notes and Souvenirs). High; two texts by the same witness agree.
- Conflicts: In the Process 1911 Thérèse "heard" the remark herself; in 1915 and in the notes the words "had been reported" to her. The first remark ("how little you are loved") is dropped in PA 1915. F-EW-1719 (later chunk) gives the speaker's later fate. Compare F-EW-1436.
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high: joy at being judged a poor nun.

### E-P6-253 — 1897, infirmary — "A little blade of moss" in heaven
- What happened: When TSA spoke of the glory Thérèse would have in heaven, Thérèse said it would not be as TSA thought. She had asked God to be "a little nothing". A gardener making a bouquet fills the gaps between fine flowers with moss, and she would be a little blade of moss among God's beautiful flowers.
- Cards: F-EW-1316
- Best details:
  - « un petit brin de mousse parmi les belles fleurs du bon Dieu » (F-EW-1316).
- Evidence: TSA (heard; 1911 and 1915). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high: a gardener's image for her place in heaven.

### E-P6-254 — 1897, infirmary — Refusing to "throw flowers" at the community
- What happened: With the community gathered by her bed, Mère Agnès said: "What if you threw flowers to the community!" Thérèse answered: oh no, little mother, don't ask that; I do not want to throw flowers to creatures. She would do it for the Virgin and St Joseph, but not for other creatures. Céline adds that she refused when asked to throw roses at a Sister as a sign of affection. Marie des Anges's record (Dernières paroles à d'autres soeurs) dates the "no flowers to creatures" saying to September 1897.
- Cards: F-EW-1322, F-EW-1358, F-OW-328
- Best details:
  - « je ne veux pas jeter de fleurs aux créatures » (F-EW-1322).
  - Date September 1897 (F-OW-328).
- Evidence: MdA (probably present, not stated; Process 1911); Céline (saw and heard). Medium (MdA), high (Céline). | Marie des Anges (recorder). Medium.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-255 — said 1897 (reported 1910) — She foresaw pilgrimages and asked that her things be kept
- What happened: Marie du Sacré-Cœur and Geneviève told Taylor that near the end Thérèse foresaw that people would come on pilgrimage and that her smallest objects should be kept. Taylor was sure of the words about objects, less sure about "pilgrimage".
- Cards: F-EW-1330
- Best details:
  - « qu'il fallait garder les moindres objets qui avaient été à son usage » (F-EW-1330).
- Evidence: Taylor, heard it from the two Sisters (Process 1910). Medium: second-hand, and Taylor qualified the wording. The judge pressed on whether these were Thérèse's literal words.
- Conflicts: "pilgrimage" may be the Sisters' interpretation.
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-256 — 1897, infirmary — "On earth I do not know you": Thérèse and TSA
- What happened: When TSA asked to be helped at her death and spared purgatory, Thérèse said she would come and that she would not go to purgatory, being so faithful. She added: what joy to see soon all the beauties of your soul, to know you as you are, "for on earth I do not know you".
- Cards: F-EW-1345
- Best details:
  - « vous connaître telle que vous êtes et je m'en réjouis, car sur la terre je ne vous connais pas. » (F-EW-1345).
- Evidence: TSA (Souvenirs §19). High.
- Conflicts: The pages never say that TSA is traditionally identified as the Sister Thérèse found antipathetic (Ms C 13v-14r); the witness sees only friendship. The remark reads differently in that light.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high: possible hidden reference to Manuscript C's antipathetic Sister.

### E-P6-257 — 1897 — "Little joys at the letter-box, by the turn"
- What happened: She told Mère Agnès: after my death you'll have little joys from me at the letter box, on the side of the turn (the revolving hatch). TSA says Thérèse foresaw that Agnès would be busy "with her little Thérèse" till the end of her life. Marie des Anges said Thérèse told Mère Agnès one day: "After my death you will have many little joys, at the letter box and toward the turn." At the time the words were mysterious. In 1915 Marie des Anges said they were "fully realised": the mail (letters, up to 500 a day) and the turn (visitors, parcels) brought the joys.
- Cards: F-EW-1351, F-EW-2525
- Best details:
  - "little joys ... at the letter box, on the side of the turn" (F-EW-1351).
  - "up to 500 letters a day" as the fulfilment (F-EW-2525).
- Evidence: Marie des Anges (hearsay). Medium. | Marie des Anges (second-hand from Agnès; PA 10 [898]). Medium. A prediction later events were judged to fulfil (M-EW-081).
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-258 — 1897, novitiate / infirmary — Correcting a novice while shaking with fever: "I must die weapons in hand"
- What happened: In her last months, trembling with fever and "throat on fire", Thérèse found all her strength to rebuke and correct a novice. She then told Céline she must die "weapons in hand", with the sword of the Spirit (a phrase from the Carmelite Rule).
- Cards: F-EW-1353
- Best details:
  - « Il faut que je meure les armes à la main » (F-EW-1353).
- Evidence: Céline (saw and heard; Conseils et Souvenirs). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial (the phrase is in Last Conversations)
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-259 — 1897, infirmary — How to kiss the crucifix
- What happened: During her illness, after Céline had been imperfect, Thérèse told her to kiss her crucifix. Céline kissed the feet. Thérèse said: "Is that where a child kisses her Father? Quick, the face!" Then she made Céline put the crucifix on her own cheek "to be kissed" and said all was forgotten. The Carnet jaune dates the scene to 2 Aug: "I have no great desires for Heaven; I'll be glad to go, that's all!" "No one will be able to say of me: 'She is dying of not dying.'" To Céline, who kissed the feet of the crucifix: "You're not following 'baby's' doctrine! Kiss him quickly on both cheeks and make him kiss you." She said she felt "very lively joy" not only when found imperfect but at feeling it herself: "that is better than all the compliments, which bore me".
- Cards: F-EW-1355, F-OW-268
- Best details:
  - "Is that where a child kisses her Father? Quick, the face!" (F-EW-1355).
  - « Baisez-le bien vite sur les deux joues et faites-vous embrasser. » (F-OW-268).
  - "No one will be able to say of me: 'She is dying of not dying.'" (F-OW-268).
- Evidence: Céline (experienced; Conseils et Souvenirs). High. In Last Conversations, Céline's notes. | Mère Agnès (CJ 2.8.3-6). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: Date 2 Aug (CJ); Céline's own accounts undated (August).
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-260 — 1897, infirmary — "I die in a bed! I wanted to die in an arena"
- What happened: Grieved by the persecution of religious communities, she still brightened at the thought they might shed their blood. In her last illness Céline heard her exclaim: "When I think that I am dying in a bed! I would so have liked to die in an arena!" Céline's 1910 account puts the same "arena" saying in the funeral preparations in July-August 1897.
- Cards: F-EW-1356, F-EW-1519
- Best details:
  - "When I think that I am dying in a bed! I would so have liked to die in an arena!" (F-EW-1356).
  - Same saying: dying in a bed instead of an arena (F-EW-1519).
- Evidence: Céline (heard). High. | Céline (heard, 1910). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-261 — summer 1897 — Intestinal tuberculosis, gangrene and bedsores
- What happened: Céline states that to the lung disease was added tuberculosis of the intestines, which caused gangrene, while sores formed from extreme thinness. They could not relieve them. More on the last weeks. She became so thin that in several places the bones broke through the skin, and two very painful sores formed. The last weeks were atrocious: to the lung disease was added tuberculosis of the intestines, which brought gangrene. In the last days her sputum was purulent with caseous (cheese-like) matter, giving off a smell that strongly showed the organ was decomposing. The Carmel editors' August health note: haemoptysis stopped 5 Aug; pain in side, shoulder and right arm; breathlessness daily from 11 a.m. to midnight. From 15 Aug oppression, left-side pain, swollen legs, fever, could not rise alone. On 17 Aug "tuberculosis at the last degree": left lung one-third affected, right lung wholly; hours of coughing, headaches, bedsores from emaciation, intestinal pain "to scream", fear of gangrene, burning thirst, hard swollen abdomen, very red urine. On 30 Aug left lung half affected. On 22-23 Aug she said: "Well, it's better, if one has to suffer much and everywhere, to have several illnesses together. It's like a journey, where one puts up with all sorts of discomforts, knowing it will end soon." "There are only the hands left!" The milk diet stopped 23 Aug.
- Cards: F-EW-1364, F-EW-1588, F-EW-1590, F-EW-1601, F-EW-1595, F-OW-299
- Best details:
  - Sores from extreme thinness that could not be relieved (F-EW-1364).
  - Bones broke through the skin in several places (F-EW-1588).
  - Sputum purulent and cheese-like, smelling of decay (F-EW-1590).
  - « Il n'y a plus que les mains !... » (F-OW-299).
  - "It's like a journey, where one puts up with all sorts of discomforts, knowing it will end soon" (F-OW-299).
- Evidence: Céline, second infirmarian. Medium: lay diagnosis, but from the closest nurse. | Mère Agnès (saw, 1915); Céline (infirmary aide). High for symptoms; medium for the blame on Gonzague (editors). | Carmel editors' note (scholarship); Mère Agnès (CJ 22.8.2, 22.8.4, 22.8.10; 23.8.10). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: The editors' "left lung one-third, right lung wholly" differs from Pauline's "both lungs affected" (F-EW-1573).
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-262 — said August 1897, Carmel — The Divine Office: "my happiness and my martyrdom"
- What happened: She was happy and proud to be hebdomadary, saying the prayers aloud in the choir like the priest, and to read the Gospel as first chantress. She said the Divine Office was both her happiness and her martyrdom, because she wanted to say it without error but sometimes made mistakes despite her care. More detail on 6 Aug 1897. She said she was proud when she was hebdomadary, saying the prayers aloud in the middle of choir, because the priest said the same prayers at Mass and she had, like him, the right to speak aloud before the Blessed Sacrament, give blessings and absolutions, and read the Gospel as first chantress. Céline says she said on her deathbed she did not think anyone could have wanted more than she to say the Office well. She taught novices to compose their bearing at Office because of its dignity. The Carnet jaune (6 Aug) repeats: "How proud I was when I was hebdomadary at the Office... The Office was both my happiness and my martyrdom." She sometimes, from distraction, let her part pass "without opening my mouth". "I excuse Sisters who forget or make mistakes very much."
- Cards: F-EW-1511, F-EW-1536, F-EW-1540, F-OW-276
- Best details:
  - Happiness and martyrdom at once because of mistakes (F-EW-1511).
  - « Que j'étais fière quand j'étais semainière à l'Office! » (F-EW-1536).
  - "I excuse Sisters who forget or make mistakes very much" (F-OW-276).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High. | Mère Agnès (heard, NV f. 274r-v); Céline (1915). High. | Mère Agnès (CJ 6.8.6). Medium-high. Links to her wish to be a priest (Ms B 2v).
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-263 — August 1897 — She rejects a priest's claim that the Virgin did not suffer physically
- What happened: A priest wrote to the Carmel that the Virgin did not know bodily suffering. Thérèse said that looking at Mary's statue that evening she understood this was false. Mary suffered from travel, cold, heat, tiredness and fasting. She liked to imagine the Holy Family's life as very ordinary, not full of the marvels people tell.
- Cards: F-EW-1512
- Best details:
  - Mary "suffered from travel, cold, heat, tiredness and fasting" (F-EW-1512).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-264 — August 1897, infirmary — "Leave me my little shoulder pain"
- What happened: In the infirmary they guessed at last that she suffered greatly in one shoulder and wanted to ease it. She said to leave her "little shoulder pain", because it made her think of Jesus carrying the cross. On 3 Aug 1897 she said her shoulder was very bruised. When a Sister offered to put cotton wool on it, she said no: they must not take away her little cross.
- Cards: F-EW-1513, F-EW-1525
- Best details:
  - "little shoulder pain" (F-EW-1513).
  - "my little cross" (F-EW-1525).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High. Note "they guessed at last": her pain was not noticed. | Mère Agnès (saw and heard; NV f. 271r). High. The cause of the bruise is not stated.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-265 — 2 August 1897, infirmary — "You dither far too much, my little Mother"
- What happened: Mère Agnès said she wanted to have Thérèse's heart preserved, like Mère Geneviève's. Thérèse said "Do as you like!" When Agnès changed her mind because the idea repelled her, Thérèse looked rather sad and said: "You dither far too much, my little Mother; I've noticed it many times in my life..."
- Cards: F-OW-267
- Best details:
  - « Vous tergiversez bien trop, ma petite mère, je l'ai remarqué bien des fois dans ma vie... » (F-OW-267).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 2.8.1). Medium-high. Not found in Novissima Verba. Frank criticism of Pauline, recorded by Pauline herself.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-266 — 3 Aug 1897, infirmary — "Do not lead the family life" after I am gone
- What happened: Coming out of the parlour with Pauline and her two other sisters, Thérèse told them to be careful about regular observance. After a parlour they should not stop to talk among themselves, because that is like being at home. When she was gone, they must not lead "family life" among themselves, nor tell each other parlour news without permission, and ask it only for useful, not amusing, things. The Carnet jaune confirms the 3 Aug warning: after a parlour visit, do not stop and chat among yourselves, "for then it's like being at home".
- Cards: F-EW-1524, F-EW-1526, F-EW-1527, F-EW-1529, F-OW-269
- Best details:
  - « faites bien attention à ne pas mener la vie de famille » (F-EW-1527).
  - « Quand je serai partie, faites bien attention à ne pas mener la vie de famille » (F-EW-1529).
  - "for then it's like being at home" (F-OW-269).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; 1910, 1915); Céline (heard; 1910, 1915). High. A response to community criticism of the "Martin clan": links to the "four sisters" complaint (F-EW-469). | Mère Agnès (CJ 3.8). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-267 — 3 Aug 1897 (note); Sept 1897 (plea) — The note of 3 August and the September plea
- What happened: After a crisis the Sisters saw her suddenly moved, "angelic". Too moved to explain, she gave Céline that evening a note dated 3 Aug 1897 quoting Psalm 22 ("I fear no evil"). One September morning she begged Céline to pray to the Virgin for her: she would pray so much if Céline were ill; "for oneself one doesn't dare ask". LT 262 (3 Aug 1897) is the note itself: "O my God, how sweet you are to the little victim of your Merciful Love! Now that you join outward suffering to the trials of my soul, I cannot say 'the anguish of death has surrounded me' but I cry in my gratitude: 'I have gone down into the valley of the shadow of death, yet I fear no evil: because you are with me, Lord!'" The note is dated "3 August 1897 - Ps XXII. 4".
- Cards: F-EW-1528, F-OW-515
- Best details:
  - « soi-même, on n'ose pas demander! » (F-EW-1528).
  - « Maintenant que Vous joignez la souffrance extérieure aux épreuves de mon âme » (F-OW-515).
- Evidence: Céline (received the note; heard; Process 1910). Medium on date. | Her own writing (LT 262). High. One of the few notes joining "outward suffering" to the trial of faith in the same sentence, dated. Absent from the 1911 edition.
- Conflicts: Date conflict inside the source: Céline says September; the cited notebook date is 23 Aug.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-268 — 3 August 1897, infirmary — "I will die of death"
- What happened: No one understood the course of her illness; a Sister asked, "What will you die of, then?" "Why, I'll die of death! Didn't God tell Adam what he would die of: 'You shall die the death'? That's all." "I have suffered a long time, but little sufferings. Since 28 July they have been great sufferings." Her shoulder was bruised; offered cotton wool: "No, you mustn't take my little cross away."
- Cards: F-OW-269
- Best details:
  - « Mais, je mourrai de mort ! » (F-OW-269).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 3.8.6-9). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-269 — 4 Aug 1897 (DE), infirmary — "No, I am not a saint"
- What happened: When she said she had refused God nothing since age three, Céline cried: you see, you are a saint! She answered quickly: no, I am not a saint. I have never done the actions of the saints; I am a very little soul God has filled with graces. You will see in heaven I speak the truth. Céline's 1910 card puts it with the "too little to be damned" saying and the crown answer to the superior.
- Cards: F-EW-1533, F-EW-1518
- Best details:
  - « Non, je ne suis pas une sainte, je n'ai jamais fait les actions des saints » (F-EW-1533).
- Evidence: Céline (involved; 1910, 1915). High. Counterweight to "little saint" (F-EW-1520).
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-270 — 4 Aug 1897, infirmary — The dream of war against the Prussians
- What happened: She dozed off for a second during prayer and dreamed that soldiers were lacking for a war against the Prussians. Pauline said "send Sr Thérèse". She agreed, though she preferred a holy war. Awake, she said with animation she would have loved to fight heretics in the Crusades, unafraid of bullets or fire. She asked if it was possible she would die in a bed.
- Cards: F-EW-1530
- Best details:
  - « Est-ce possible que je meure dans un lit? » (F-EW-1530).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; NV f. 271v). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-271 — July and 4 Aug 1897, infirmary — A palm to be dropped "to scatter graces"; the ear of wheat
- What happened: In July 1897 they told her a palm would be put in her hand after death. She said yes, but she would have to let go of it to scatter graces on earth with full hands. On 4 August, holding the finest of some ears of wheat brought to the infirmary, she said it was the image of her soul, loaded with graces for herself and many others.
- Cards: F-EW-1531
- Best details:
  - "let go of the palm to scatter graces" (F-EW-1531).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High. Also in Last Conversations.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-272 — 1890s; words 4 Aug 1897 — Hebrew and Greek; Gospels bound to wear on her heart
- What happened: Differences between Bible translations saddened her. She said that if she had been a priest she would have studied Hebrew and Greek thoroughly to know God's thought. She wanted to carry the Gospel on her heart. She worked hard to find the Gospels printed separately, had them bound, and got copies for Céline too. The Carnet jaune (4 Aug): "It's only in Heaven that we'll see the truth about everything... Even for Holy Scripture, isn't it sad to see all the differences of translation! If I had been a priest I would have learned Hebrew and Greek; I would not have been satisfied with Latin; that way I would have known the real text dictated by the Holy Spirit." She dreamed she was sent to a war and said she would have loved to go on crusade: "I wouldn't have been afraid of catching a bullet!" "And I who wanted martyrdom, is it possible I will die in a bed!"
- Cards: F-EW-1532, F-OW-270
- Best details:
  - « Si j'avais été prêtre, j'aurais étudié à fond l'hébreu et le grec » (F-EW-1532).
  - « Si j'avais été prêtre, j'aurais appris l'hébreu et le grec » (F-OW-270).
- Evidence: Céline (saw and heard, 1910). High. | Mère Agnès (CJ 4.8.5-7). Medium-high. Céline's Varia n. 11: "When I think that I'm dying in a bed! I would have liked to die in an arena."
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-273 — 4 August 1897, infirmary — "My little life is to suffer, and that's it"
- What happened: Asked how she arranged her "little life" now: "My little life is to suffer, and that's it! I couldn't say: My God, this is for the Church, my God, this is for France... It would tire me too much to tell him: Give this to Peter, give this to Paul." For her missionary brothers she says simply: "My God, give them all that I want for myself." "No, I don't think I'm a great saint! I think I'm a very little saint."
- Cards: F-OW-271
- Best details:
  - « Ma petite vie, c'est de souffrir et puis ça y est ! » (F-OW-271).
  - "I think I'm a very little saint" (F-OW-271).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 4.8.2, 4.8.8). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: Compare "No, I am not a saint" (see E-P6-269).
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-274 — 4 August 1897, infirmary — The sparrow's egg: a "conjuring trick" for the saints
- What happened: Céline said it was impossible for a sparrow's egg (herself) to hatch a bird as lovely as Thérèse. Thérèse: "Yes, but! I'll do a conjuring trick to amuse all the saints." She would whisper to God, "Change the nature of the little bird by blowing on it", have the Virgin kiss it and Joseph caress it, make all the saints say they love it as much as her, then "with a triumphant air I'll break the little egg and a pretty little bird will come and sit beside me on God's knees". Céline notes her "oui, mais !" was a phrase "peculiar to her".
- Cards: F-OW-272
- Best details:
  - « je ferai un tour de physique pour amuser tous les saints » (F-OW-272).
- Evidence: Céline (Dernières paroles à Céline, 4 Août n. 4; 3 Septembre n. 2). Medium.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-275 — 5 Aug 1897, infirmary — A prioress should look as if she has no troubles
- What happened: Replying to a confidence from Pauline, she said a prioress should always let others think she has no troubles. She should avoid saying things like "you have problems, I have them too with such-and-such a sister".
- Cards: F-EW-1534
- Best details:
  - Advice to Pauline about her own future office (F-EW-1534).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; NV f. 273r). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-276 — 4 June and 5 Aug 1897, Lisieux Carmel — She wanted no visions and expected no signs after death
- What happened: She told her sisters not to be surprised if she did not appear to them after death or if they saw nothing extraordinary, because her "little way" was to desire to see nothing. She said images of angels did her no good; she could feed only on truth, so she never desired visions. The Carnet jaune puts the "don't be surprised if I don't appear" saying on 4 June (CJ 4.6.1) and the images-of-angels saying on 5 Aug (CJ 5.8.2-4): "All these images do me no good; I can feed only on truth. That's why I never wanted visions."
- Cards: F-EW-1535, F-OW-237, F-OW-273
- Best details:
  - "her little way was to desire to see nothing" (F-EW-1535).
  - « I can feed only on truth » (F-OW-273).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; NV f. 251r-v and f. 272r). High. Contrasts with the many later reported apparitions. | Mère Agnès (Carnet jaune). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-277 — night of 5-6 Aug 1897, infirmary — The Holy Face beside her bed and her worst temptation
- What happened: On the feast of the Transfiguration they left a large image of the Holy Face near her bed, with flowers and lit by a night-light. She never suffered more from the temptation against faith than that night. She said she had been so tempted, but had kept looking at the Holy Face and making acts of faith. After the night waiting to die she softly sang to the Virgin's statue "Quand viendra-t-il, ma tendre Mère". The Carnet jaune adds that she kept watch all night, looking at the Holy Face, pushing away temptations and making acts of faith (see E-P6-284).
- Cards: F-EW-1539, F-EW-1584, F-OW-275
- Best details:
  - The image lit by a night-light, flowers, the Transfiguration (F-EW-1539).
  - "Ah, I made many acts of faith!" (F-OW-275).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; 1915). High. | Mère Agnès (CJ 6.8). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-278 — 30 July-Aug 1897, Lisieux Carmel — "I believe she will die laughing"
- What happened: Marie wrote that Thérèse "amuses herself telling us all that will happen after her death. From the way she tells it, where one should weep, one laughs aloud, so amusing is she... She reviews everything, it is her happiness, and shares it in forms that make us laugh a lot. I think she will die laughing, she is so gay." The threat then seeming to pass, M. and Mme Guérin took their annual cure at Vichy. At the Carmel Gonzague's name day was celebrated four days early so Thérèse could take part, and it was Thérèse who handed the prioress, with a charming mime, the gift her aunt brought from Évreux.
- Cards: F-EW-2632
- Best details:
  - "I think she will die laughing, she is so gay" (F-EW-2632).
  - Gonzague's name day held four days early, so that Thérèse could share it (F-EW-2632).
- Evidence: Piat (1967), quoting Marie's letters. Medium-high. Other cards record her laughter about her own funeral plans.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-279 — 5 August 1897, infirmary — "A thread on my leg" and "the little grey mouse"
- What happened: Unable to move: "David said in the psalms: 'I am like the grasshopper that keeps changing place.' Well, I can't say as much! I'd like to go for a walk, but I have a thread on my leg!" "When the saints have shut the door of Heaven on me, they will sing: 'At last we've got you, little grey mouse, at last we've got you and we'll keep you!'" (a little song that came back to her). When Marie told her she would see resplendent angels at her death: "All these images do me no good; I can feed only on truth. That's why I never wanted visions."
- Cards: F-OW-273
- Best details:
  - « Enfin nous te tenons, / Petite souris grise » (F-OW-273).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 5.8.2-4). Medium-high. The grey mouse and "fil à la patte" are not found in Novissima Verba.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-280 — 5 Aug 1897 (recalling 26 Feb 1895) — The Holy Face and how she wrote "Vivre d'amour"
- What happened: For the Transfiguration (6 Aug) the Sisters hung the choir's picture of the Holy Face by her bed. She said: "How well Our Lord did to lower his eyes to give us his portrait! Since the eyes are the mirror of the soul, if we had seen his soul we would have died of joy." "While I was composing my hymn 'Living by Love' it helped me write it very easily. I wrote from memory, during my evening silence, the 15 stanzas I had composed without a draft during the day." After composing the stanza "Living by love is to wipe your Face", she said it to the picture in passing "and wept with love". Isaiah 53 ("without beauty") "was the whole basis of my devotion to the Holy Face, or rather of all my piety".
- Cards: F-OW-274
- Best details:
  - « J'ai écrit de mémoire, pendant mon silence du soir les 15 couplets que j'avais composés, sans brouillon, dans la journée. » (F-OW-274).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 5.8.7, 5.8.9). Medium-high. Detail on her working method as a poet (how PN 17 was written).
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-281 — Aug 1897 (DE 6 Aug), infirmary — The old infirmarian's handling that "could have had grave consequences"
- What happened: The prioress asked Thérèse to explain a way of acting by the first infirmarian (an elderly nun) which "could have had serious drawbacks for her condition". Thérèse told the whole truth but chose a more charitable expression. The Carnet jaune names the infirmarian and the fault: Sr Saint-Stanislas, her first infirmarian, left her during Vespers with the door and window open; the draught was strong. The prioress was displeased and asked for explanations. Thérèse told Mère Agnès she told the truth, but "a kinder way of putting it came to mind than the one I was going to use".
- Cards: F-EW-1508, F-OW-278
- Best details:
  - She told the whole truth but chose a more charitable expression (F-EW-1508).
  - « il m'est venu à la pensée une expression plus charitable que celle dont j'allais me servir » (F-OW-278).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard Thérèse; NV f. 274v). Medium: the infirmarian's act is not described. | Mère Agnès (CJ 6.8.7). Medium-high. Nursing care in the infirmary was sometimes careless.
- Conflicts: This file does not name the infirmarian; other sources name Sr Saint-Stanislas. | NV card (F-EW-1508) does not describe the act; here it is the open window and door.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-282 — 6 Aug 1897, infirmary — "How little regularity there is here!"
- What happened: About things confided to her, she said few religious are perfect. Many act carelessly, telling themselves they are not bound. Those who do everything as well as possible are rare. She stressed silence above all, since it is the point most failed. When she was on the week for the lesser offices she took such care that Céline was surprised. Thérèse answered sadly: oh, how few holy nuns there are! She begged Céline not to be one of the careless ones.
- Cards: F-EW-1537, F-EW-1538, F-EW-1541
- Best details:
  - « Oh! qu'il y a peu de régularité ici! » (F-EW-1538).
  - « Oh! qu'il y a peu de saintes religieuses! Qu'il y en a peu qui ne font pas tout n'importe comment! » (F-EW-1541).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; 1910, 1915); Céline (heard, 1915). High. A critical view of the community from Thérèse herself.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-283 — July-Sept 1897, infirmary — Hymns and verses she sang or recited when ill
- What happened: On 11 July she recited a stanza of her own poem to Mary ("Pourquoi je t'aime, ô Marie", PN 54) while tempted against faith. On 14 July she repeated a stanza of "Rappelle-toi" (PN 24). On 6 Aug, after a night waiting to die, she softly sang to the Virgin's statue "Quand viendra-t-il, ma tendre Mère" (from "Soupir de l'exilé", in the Paris hymnbook "Chants à Marie", 1879). On 1 Aug she quoted her poem PN 45. On 14 Sept she quoted her rose poem (PN 34) while unpetalling a rose over her crucifix. On 23 Aug she said she loved to sing to Mary: "L'étroit chemin du ciel, tu l'as rendu facile".
- Cards: F-EW-1584
- Best details:
  - "L'étroit chemin du ciel, tu l'as rendu facile" (F-EW-1584).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (saw and heard; PO 1 f. 170v; NV). High. The hymnbook source is given by the editors' note.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-284 — 6 August 1897, infirmary — Waiting for death all night; "like a little Robinson on his island"
- What happened: She hoped to die in the night of 5-6 Aug: "I kept watch all night, like the little girl in the song of the little Christmas shoe... I didn't stop looking at the Holy Face... I pushed away many temptations... Ah, I made many acts of faith!" After Dr de Cornière found her as ill but doubted death was near: "I'm like a poor little Robinson on his island... they announce a ship... I stay on the shore, I look far off... and, seeing nothing on the horizon, I tell myself: They've tricked me! I'm not going to leave!"
- Cards: F-OW-275
- Best details:
  - « Ils m'ont trompée ! Je ne vais pas m'en aller ! » (F-OW-275).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 6.8.1-2). Medium-high. The Robinson image is not found in Novissima Verba.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-285 — 6 August 1897 — What "staying a little child" means, in her own words
- What happened: Asked at Matins what she meant by "remaining a little child before God": "It is to recognise one's nothingness, to expect everything from God as a little child expects everything from its father; not to worry about anything, not to earn a fortune... As soon as the child grows up his father... says: Work now, you can support yourself. It's so as not to hear that that I did not want to grow up, feeling incapable of earning my living, the eternal life of Heaven." It also means "not to be discouraged by one's faults, for children fall often, but they are too small to hurt themselves much."
- Cards: F-OW-277
- Best details:
  - « C'est pour ne pas entendre cela que je n'ai pas voulu grandir » (F-OW-277).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 6.8.8). Medium-high. A version is in Novissima Verba.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-286 — 7 August 1897, infirmary — The little white flower from her father, glued to a picture
- What happened: She showed Mère Agnès the photograph of Notre-Dame des Victoires on which she had glued the little white flower her father gave her the day she told him her vocation (29 May 1887). The root had come off, "and the Child Jesus seems to hold it in his hand and smile at it". "That the little flower has lost its root will tell you I'm in Heaven."
- Cards: F-OW-279
- Best details:
  - « Que la petite fleur ait perdu sa racine, ça vous dira que je suis au Ciel... » (F-OW-279).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 7.8.3). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: Ms A 50v says she put the flower in her Imitation; by 1897 it was on the picture.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-287 — 8 Aug 1897 — Childhood pity for servants; "one should hide to eat"
- What happened: On 8 August 1897 she said that as a child she pitied people who served. The difference between masters and servants proved to her that there is a heaven where each will be placed by inner merit. Speaking of childhood, she said that if Jesus and Mary had not taken part in feasts she would never have understood inviting friends to table. She thought one should hide to eat, or at least stay with the family. The Carnet jaune (8 Aug) gives the servants passage in full: "I felt great pity for the people who served at big dinners. If by bad luck they let a few drops fall on the tablecloth or on a guest, I saw the lady of the house look at them severely; then those poor people blushed with shame, and I said to myself, inwardly quite revolted: Oh! how this difference between masters and servants here below proves there is a heaven where each will be placed according to inner merit."
- Cards: F-EW-1542, F-EW-1543, F-OW-281
- Best details:
  - "one should hide to eat" (F-EW-1543). Pauline never saw her commit the smallest act of greed.
  - « je me disais, toute révoltée intérieurement » (F-OW-281).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High. | Mère Agnès (CJ 8.8.4). Medium-high. A social observation from a bourgeois girl; useful "world around her" detail.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-288 — 8 August 1897, infirmary — Looking at the sky: "the other is more and more closed to me"
- What happened: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur said, "How lovingly you look at the sky!" Thérèse only smiled; later she told Mère Agnès: "Ah, she thinks I'm looking at the firmament thinking of the real Heaven! But no, it's simply because I admire the material sky; the other is more and more closed to me." Then she told herself it was by love that she looked, "since everything I do... since my offering, is by love".
- Cards: F-OW-280
- Best details:
  - « l'autre m'est de plus en plus fermé » (F-OW-280).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 8.8.2). Medium-high. Trial of faith in her last weeks.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-289 — June-July 1897 (DE date 9 Aug), Lisieux Carmel — Pauline's "permission" to cut or add
- What happened: While writing Manuscript C in 1897, Thérèse told Pauline that anything Pauline chose to cut or add, Thérèse herself cut or added, and that she must have no scruple. On a day of many interruptions she said her pages on charity were badly written and that Pauline would have to retouch everything.
- Cards: F-EW-1423
- Best details:
  - « tout ce que vous trouverez bon de retrancher ou d'ajouter au cahier de ma vie, c'est moi qui le retranche et qui l'ajoute » (F-EW-1423).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1910). Medium: only Pauline reports it, and it justifies her own editing. See gaps.
- Conflicts: Self-serving statement from the editor of the text. Compare F-EW-1407 (she knew in June 1897 that Pauline would publish).
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-290 — 9 August 1897, infirmary — "I will die with weapons in hand"; "something for every taste"
- What happened: Mère Agnès said, "Our warrior is struck down!" Thérèse: "I'm not a warrior who fought with earthly weapons but with 'the sword of the spirit which is the word of God'. So illness could not strike me down, and only last night I used my sword with a novice. I've said it: I will die with weapons in hand." About her manuscript: "There will be something for every taste, except for extraordinary ways." Told she was a saint: "No, I'm not a saint; I've never done the actions of saints. I'm a very little soul whom God has filled with graces."
- Cards: F-OW-282
- Best details:
  - « Il y en aura pour tous les goûts, excepté pour les voies extraordinaires. » (F-OW-282).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 9.8.1-3). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-291 — 10-26 Aug 1897, infirmary — She asks God not to hear prayers for her relief
- What happened: Marie told her she had asked God that Thérèse not suffer much, and "this is how He answers me!" Thérèse said she had asked God not to listen to prayers that might block His plans for her. She said one cannot know what such suffering is without feeling it. On 22 August, told they prayed in vain, she said the more deaf the saints seem, the more she loves them. She had told God she wanted the prayers for her to go to sinners, not to her relief. On 26 Aug she said she was glad she had not asked for suffering; if she had, she would fear lacking patience. Marie's letter of 27 Aug (via Piat) adds: Thérèse said the previous day: "Fortunately I did not ask for suffering, for if I had asked it, I would fear not to have patience to bear it; whereas, coming from the pure will of God, he cannot refuse me patience and grace." Oppression made her suffer, but the worst torture was bowel pains. She said: "I ask that all the prayers made for me should not serve to lighten my sufferings but be all for sinners." From this time the letters became rare and news was given orally at the turn or parlour.
- Cards: F-EW-1545, F-EW-1546, F-EW-1566, F-EW-1569, F-EW-2637
- Best details:
  - « J'ai demandé que jamais les créatures ne mettent obstacle à sa volonté sur moi » (F-EW-1545).
  - « On ne sait pas ce que c'est de souffrir comme cela, non! il faut le sentir » (F-EW-1546).
  - « Heureusement que je n'ai pas demandé la souffrance » (F-EW-1569, letter of 27 Aug).
  - Bowel pains as the worst torture (F-EW-2637).
- Evidence: Marie (heard; 1910, 1915); Marie de l'Eucharistie (letter of 27 Aug 1897). High. | Piat (1967), quoting Marie's letter. Medium-high. The bowel pain matches the doctors' reports of intestinal tuberculosis.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-292 — 10 Aug 1897, infirmary — "If you had not brought me up well"
- What happened: Told that souls like hers could see their beauty safely, she said she saw no beauty, only graces. Turning to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur and Pauline, very moved, she thanked them: if they had not brought her up well, they would see something sad in her.
- Cards: F-EW-1544
- Best details:
  - "if they had not brought her up well, they would see something sad in her" (F-EW-1544).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (present, 1915). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-293 — Aug-Sept 1897, infirmary — Théophane Vénard's picture on her bed curtain
- What happened: A picture of the martyr Théophane Vénard (MEP, beheaded in Tonkin 1861), showing him pointing to Heaven, was pinned to her bed curtains. "Do you think he knows you?" "Look what he's showing me... He might well not have taken that pose..." "Each time I look at him, he looks at me too, and seems to watch me out of the corner of his eye with a half-mischievous air." She caressed the picture on both cheeks because she could not reach to kiss it. On 17 Aug she put a periwinkle on it (Mère Agnès kept it). She said she could no longer look at it without crying. "Watch out for little Théophane!" she said when Céline fixed the pillows. On 6 Sep she wept with joy at receiving a relic of Vénard.
- Cards: F-OW-283
- Best details:
  - « il semble me guetter du coin de l'oeil d'un air à moitié malin » (F-OW-283).
  - "Watch out for little Théophane!" (F-OW-283).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 10.8, 17.8, 19.8, 6.9). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial (Vénard is named in Novissima Verba)
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-294 — 29-30 July 1897; told 10 Aug 1897, Carmel of Lisieux — LT 263 to Bellière: "my passport for Heaven" (29 July) and extreme unction on the 30th
- What happened: On 10 Aug 1897 Thérèse writes: "I am now quite ready to leave; I have received my passport for Heaven, and it is my dear father who obtained this grace for me: on the 29th he gave me the guarantee that I would soon join him." (29 July is the date of her father's death in 1894; the letter does not say so.) "The next day, the doctor, astonished at the progress the illness had made in two days, told our good Mother that it was time to fulfil my desires by having me receive Extreme Unction. I had this joy on the 30th, and also the joy of seeing Jesus-Host leave the tabernacle for me, whom I received as Viaticum of my long journey!... This Bread of Heaven strengthened me; see, my pilgrimage seems unable to end. Far from complaining, I am glad the good God lets me suffer still for love of him; how sweet it is to abandon oneself in his arms, without fears or desires."
- Cards: F-OW-516
- Best details:
  - « j'ai reçu mon passeport pour le Ciel et c'est mon père chéri qui m'a obtenu cette grâce, le 29 il m'a donné la garantie que j'irai bientôt le rejoindre » (F-OW-516).
- Evidence: Her own writing (LT 263). High. "Passeport pour le Ciel" is absent from the 1911 edition.
- Conflicts: The 29 July of the letter is the third anniversary of her father's death. The doctor's remark is quoted through the prioress. The letter does not say what the "guarantee" was (a dream, a feeling or a sign).
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-295 — 10 August 1897, Carmel of Lisieux (infirmary) — Bellière's "inheritance": a reliquary, a worn crucifix, and the last picture she painted
- What happened: In LT 263 she lists what the prioress will give Bellière after her death: "1° The reliquary I received the day of my clothing, which has never left me since. 2) A little Crucifix that is incomparably dearer to me than the big one... It is not beautiful, the figure of Christ has almost disappeared; you will not be surprised when you know that since I was 13 this souvenir from one of my sisters has followed me everywhere. It was above all during my journey in Italy that this Crucifix became precious to me; I had it touch all the important relics I had the happiness to venerate; moreover it was blessed by the Holy Father. Since I have been ill I hold our dear little Crucifix almost always in my hands." The prioress will also give him "the last picture I painted". She thanks him for his photograph.
- Cards: F-OW-517
- Best details:
  - « Il n'est pas beau, la figure du Christ a presque disparu ... depuis l'âge de 13 ans ce souvenir d'une de mes sœurs m'a suivie partout » (F-OW-517).
- Evidence: Her own writing (LT 263). High. A rare inventory of her physical objects; absent from the 1911 edition.
- Conflicts: The letter does not say whether Bellière received them; check the Carmel's records. She died holding a small crucifix (see E-P6-392).
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-296 — 10 August 1897, Carmel of Lisieux — The Blessed "are more merciful, not more strict"; "It was by choice that I became your sister"
- What happened: In LT 263 she disagrees with Bellière's idea of Heaven: he thinks that sharing in God's justice and holiness she will not be able to excuse his faults as on earth. She answers: "Have you forgotten that I will also share in the infinite mercy of the Lord? I believe the Blessed have great compassion for our miseries, they remember that, being like us fragile and mortal, they committed the same faults and fought the same battles, and their brotherly tenderness becomes even greater than it was on earth; that is why they never cease to protect us and pray for us." A postscript: "I congratulate you on your new dignity; on the 25th, the day I keep the feast of my dear little father, I will have the happiness to keep the feast of my brother Louis de France too." She also writes: "(It is by choice that I became your sister.)"
- Cards: F-OW-518
- Best details:
  - « Oubliez-vous donc que je participerai aussi à la miséricorde infinie du Seigneur ? » (F-OW-518).
  - « (C'est par choix que je suis devenue votre sœur.) » (F-OW-518).
- Evidence: Her own writing (LT 263). High for the words; low for "Louis de France" and "nouvelle dignité" (the letter does not say what they are).
- Conflicts: "Louis de France" is absent from the 1911 edition and unexplained.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-297 — mid-Aug 1897 (DE 11 Aug), infirmary — "You know you are nursing a little saint"
- What happened: About six weeks before her death, with Marie, Mother Agnès and Sr Geneviève at her bed, Thérèse looked at them with a heavenly air and said very clearly that they knew they were nursing a little saint. Marie was so moved she moved away and did not hear anything else. Pauline says that after a silence she added that they were saints too. Céline thinks she heard it herself, but she did not note it in her notebook and admits she may have known it only from her sisters. Marie's card adds that in her last days Thérèse told her three sisters to keep carefully the rose petals she plucked over her crucifix, because later they would be useful. She spoke of the countless letters that would come after her death. Once she said, with a gracious air, "you know very well you are nursing a little saint!" Marie dates the petals 14 Sept and the "saint" saying July 1897.
- Cards: F-EW-1520, F-EW-1580, F-EW-1521, F-EW-1635
- Best details:
  - « Vous savez bien que vous soignez une petite sainte » (F-EW-1520, F-EW-1580).
  - After a silence: "you are saints too" (F-EW-1580).
  - « Mes petites sœurs, vous savez bien que vous soignez une petite sainte! » (F-EW-1635).
  - "the countless letters that would come after her death" (F-EW-1635).
- Evidence: Marie (heard, 1915); Mère Agnès (heard, 1910); Céline (uncertain, 1915). High for the words; the tribunal asked if she explained or corrected them, and Marie did not hear. | Marie (heard, 1910). High.
- Conflicts: Marie places all three sisters at the bedside; Céline is unsure she heard it. At [787] Thérèse denied being a saint. The tribunal's question shows it worried about pride. | Dating: "July 1897" for the saying (Marie) against mid-August (F-EW-1520) and DE 11 Aug (F-EW-1580).
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-298 — 11 August 1897, infirmary — "The Blessed Virgin is less happy than we are"
- What happened: "The Blessed Virgin has no Blessed Virgin to love; she is less happy than we are." (She had said this at recreation earlier.) "I often pray to the saints without being heard, but the deafer they seem to my prayers, the more I love them... Because I wanted more not to see God and the saints, and to stay in the night of faith, than others want to see and understand." "I never 'pretend'; I'm not like the wife of Jeroboam."
- Cards: F-OW-284
- Best details:
  - « La Sainte Vierge, elle n'a pas de Sainte Vierge à aimer, elle est moins heureuse que nous. » (F-OW-284).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 11.8.4-6). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-299 — 12 Aug 1897 — An image in a trembling hand for the novice's 23rd birthday
- What happened: On 12 Aug 1897, the novice's 23rd birthday, Thérèse wrote her an image in a trembling hand: may your life be all humility and love so you may soon come where I am going. A second source: the image was LT 264, with the words that MT's life should be all humility and love so that she would soon come where Thérèse was going, "into the arms of Jesus". LT 264 (12 Aug 1897, to Sr Marie de la Trinité, for her 23rd birthday): "May your life be all humility and love, so that soon you may come where I am going: into the arms of Jesus!" (holy-card dedication).
- Cards: F-EW-1378, F-EW-1548, F-OW-519
- Best details:
  - Written "in a trembling hand" (F-EW-1378).
  - "with a trembling hand" (F-EW-1548).
  - Marie de la Trinité was born on 12 Aug 1874, aged 23 (F-OW-519).
- Evidence: Marie de la Trinité. High. | MT, recipient (PO 17 f. 1095v; PA 21 p. 1278). High. | Her own writing (LT 264). High. One of her last dated dedications.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-300 — Aug 1897 (DE 12 Aug), infirmary — A sudden temptation of greed: "I feel I'm dying of hunger"
- What happened: During her last illness she was suddenly assailed by a real temptation of greed. Her imagination showed her all kinds of fine dishes and she was obsessed with wanting them. She sighed that all her life eating had been a torture, and now she felt she was dying of hunger. The Carnet jaune adds cravings (12 and 31 Aug): "It's incredible: now that I can't eat any more, I get cravings for all sorts of good things, like chicken, cutlets, the Sunday rice with sorrel, tuna!" On 31 Aug: "I have an appetite for my whole life. I always ate like a martyr, and now I'd devour everything. I feel I'm dying of hunger." She told Mère Agnès that once, to mortify herself, she thought of dirty things while eating, "but afterwards I found it simpler to offer God what I found to my taste". Céline recorded: "Food was something I couldn't think of, it turned my stomach, and now I have an animal's cravings, I'd devour anything given me, and that humiliates me a lot." Before confession on 26 Aug: "I don't know if I should tell Fr Youf I've had thoughts of gluttony, because I thought of things I like, but I offer them to God." She was annoyed on 4 Sep that her craving for a simple dish had been reported to her uncle.
- Cards: F-EW-1507, F-OW-285, F-OW-303
- Best details:
  - « il me semble que je meurs de faim! Oh! que c'est affreux de mourir de faim! » (F-EW-1507).
  - « comme du poulet, des côtelettes, du riz à l'oseille du Dimanche, du thon !... » (F-OW-285).
  - « je ne sais pas s'il faut dire à Mr Youf que j'ai eu des pensées de gourmandise » (F-OW-303).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard; 1910, f. 226v-227r). High. | Mère Agnès (CJ 12.8.5; 31.8.5, 31.8.12; 26.8); Céline (Varia 3). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes (not found in Novissima Verba)
- Scene potential: high: a dying girl tempted by dishes she cannot eat.

### E-P6-301 — 12 Aug 1897 (cross-reference), infirmary — Tears at the Confiteor before Communion
- What happened: One morning when Communion was brought to her, she felt extraordinary humility at the Confiteor. She felt like the publican, a great sinner, and wept when the Host touched her lips. She thought these were tears of perfect contrition.
- Cards: F-EW-1547
- Best details:
  - She felt like the publican (F-EW-1547).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (heard, 1915). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-302 — 12 August 1897 (recalling 1896-1897) — Why she resisted being spared community duties
- What happened: Her Sisters recalled how she had resisted their pleas to rest, not rise with the community, skip Matins. "You didn't understand me when I insisted, but it was because I felt people were trying to influence Our Mother. I wanted to tell Our Mother the whole truth, so that she would decide by herself."
- Cards: F-OW-286
- Best details:
  - « je sentais bien qu'on essayait d'influencer Notre Mère » (F-OW-286).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 12.8.4). Medium-high. Relevant to the later charge that the prioress neglected her illness: Thérèse says she chose to leave the decision to the prioress.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: medium.

### E-P6-303 — July-Sept 1897, infirmary — The box of lilies, the candle and holy water, laughing at her own funeral
- What happened: She asked to see a box of lilies that had arrived to decorate her bier and looked at them with pleasure: "It's for me!" To please her they discussed the new cemetery in front of her. One evening, when she might not survive the night, a blessed candle and holy water with sprinkler were put in the next room. She guessed, asked that they be placed where she could see them, then described with joy every detail of her burial. Her words made the Sisters laugh when they wanted to cry. In September she also had the aspergillum that would be used after her death brought to her. She said: "When I think I am dying in a bed! I'd have liked to die in an arena!" Céline's own account (Conseils et Souvenirs) puts the lilies in September 1897, artificial lilies: she wanted to see the box that had arrived to decorate her deathbed and said joyfully "It's for me!" — she could hardly believe it. Fearing she would not last the night, they prepared a blessed candle, holy-water stoup and sprinkler in the next room. She guessed and asked to have them placed where she could see them. She said: "When the 'Thief' carries me off, they'll put it in my hand, but don't give me the candlestick, it's too ugly!" She reviewed every detail of her burial so that they smiled when they wanted to cry.
- Cards: F-EW-1519, F-EW-1605, F-EW-1622, F-EW-1623
- Best details:
  - "It's for me!" about the lilies (F-EW-1519).
  - « qui nous faisaient rire quand nous aurions voulu pleurer » (F-EW-1519). Céline: "it was not we who encouraged her, but she who cheered us".
  - Box of lilies for the coffin when she would lie in the choir; the aspergillum (F-EW-1605).
  - « il ne faudra pas me donner le chandelier, il est trop laid ! » (F-EW-1623).
  - Artificial lilies (F-EW-1622).
- Evidence: Céline (saw and heard; PO 1910; PA 1915). High. | Céline (saw and heard, Conseils et Souvenirs). High.
- Conflicts: Céline's 1910 process account puts this in July-Aug; her Conseils et Souvenirs give September.
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: high: a dying girl inspects her funeral things and makes the Sisters laugh.

### E-P6-304 — 15 and 19 Aug 1897, infirmary — She asks the Virgin to stop her cough so Céline can sleep
- What happened: She said she had asked the Virgin the previous evening to stop her coughing so Sr Geneviève could sleep, "but I added: if you don't, I will love you even more". On 19 August, unable to bear it, she asked the Virgin to hold her head in her hands. Céline slept near her in the infirmary.
- Cards: F-EW-1549, F-EW-1553
- Best details:
  - "if you don't, I will love you even more" (F-EW-1549).
- Evidence: Céline (1915); Mère Agnès (heard; NV f. 279v, 280v). High.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-305 — infirmary, July-Sept 1897 — Céline woke by herself three times a night
- What happened: At night Thérèse never rang for Céline except at the last extremity, "or rather never", waiting for her to come on her own. Céline did, waking naturally three times at regular intervals and falling back asleep at once. She calls this extraordinary, since she has always had great trouble sleeping.
- Cards: F-EW-1604
- Best details:
  - Woke three times at regular intervals (F-EW-1604).
- Evidence: Céline (1915). Medium: her own impression.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: yes
- Scene potential: low.

### E-P6-306 — 15 August 1897, infirmary — "He wants to make me believe there is no Heaven"
- What happened: "I wonder how God can hold back so long from taking me... And then, you'd think he wants to make me 'believe' there's no Heaven!... And all the saints I love so much, where are they 'nesting'?... Ah! I'm not pretending, it's really true that I can't see a thing. But anyway, I must sing very loud in my heart: 'After death, life is immortal', or else it would go badly..." Asked if she was anxious about what would become of her: "Oh no..." On the new bells ringing for Vespers: "Not very beautiful!"
- Cards: F-OW-287
- Best details:
  - « on dirait qu'il veut me faire «accroire» qu'il n'y a pas de Ciel !... » (F-OW-287).
- Evidence: Mère Agnès (CJ 15.8.2, 15.8.5, 15.8.7). Medium-high.
- Conflicts: none
- New to English readers: partial
- Scene potential: high.

### E-P6-307 — 16 August 1897, infirmary — "The devil is around me"; suffering for Céline: right side and left side
- What happened: Céline found her in the morning pale and distorted with pain: "The devil is around me, I don't see him but I feel him... he torments me, he holds me as with a hand of iron to stop me taking the least relief, he increases my pains so that I despair... And I can't pray! I can only look at the Blessed Virgin and say: Jesus!" Until then she suffered mostly on the right side. God asked if she would suffer for Céline; she said yes, and "at that instant the left side was seized with an unbelievable intensity. I'm suffering for you and the devil doesn't want it!" Céline lit a blessed candle and calm returned, though not the new pain. Afterwards she called her right side "Thérèse's side" and her left "Céline's side".
- Cards: F-OW-289
- Best details:
  - « Je souffre pour vous et le démon ne veut pas ! » (F-OW-289).
  - "Thérèse's side" and "Céline's side" (F-OW-289).
